Общий прогресс22 спринтаЗадачи S780 гейтовПодробности и критерии ниже

Статус спринта и статус его задач — разные вещи. Для S7 реестр показывает 6/6 гейтов в работе, а очередь задач показывает 11/73 доставлено, 1/73 в работе (SRC-01-T12) и 61/73 ещё не начато. В строках остальных спринтов `≥1/N с Git-изменениями` означает только нижний предел: коммиты помечены ID спринта/гейта, но не task ID, поэтому нельзя точно посчитать начатые задачи или назвать их. Синий сегмент — только подтверждённый минимум; штриховка — задачи без точной связи с коммитом. `0/N начато` ставится лишь там, где очередь/реестр это подтверждают. Гейты показаны отдельно. План: 776 задач. Длительность — календарное окно между коммитами, не часы труда; доли — по добавленным и удалённым строкам. Без связанных коммитов длительность/доли неизвестны, а не равны нулю.

11/73 доставлены (15,1% по количеству); 1 в работе; 61 NOT_STARTED после SRC-01-T12 по последовательной очереди. Знаменатель 73 = SRC-01 (18) + DEC-01 (18) + WGR-01 (7) + PRV-01 (19) + QRY-01 (6) + TNT-01 (5). Статус задачи означает её acceptance и delivery, а не только наличие исходников. T01–T11 SRC-01 отмечены доставленными по текущему checkpoint; текущая задача — SRC-01-T12. Остальные 61 задача не начата: очередь требует завершить текущую SRC-01-T12 перед следующим таском.

Гейты S7: 0/6 CLOSED, 6/6 IN_PROGRESS. Это отдельный уровень: доставка задачи не закрывает родительский гейт.

Порядок: сначала завершить и доставить SRC-01-T12, затем продолжить S7 по очереди. NOT_STARTED означает, что задача ещё не начата по очереди; это не утверждение об отсутствии связанного исходного кода в репозитории. По остальным пяти гейтам связанные Git-изменения и зависимости раскрыты ниже. Ни код, ни документация сами по себе не означают приёмку гейта. Источник текущего checkpoint: docs/sdlc/STATE.md. PRG-06Gmail sync stabilizationЗАКРЫТ В РЕЕСТРЕ

Смысл / область

Close the residuals of commit 76140be. Classify 404s by call: history.list 404 means the cursor is too old and starts a full resync; messages.get 404 means the message is gone and is skipped with a tombstone; a messages.list 404 during recovery is handled. Quarantine persistent non-404 per-item failures after a bounded number of attempts so the cursor advances. Record skips and quarantines in a durable skip ledger with a metric. Alert when the cursor is older than 600 s.

Фаза/Phase P0 · Lane L-CONN: Connectors and ingest · Tier A · Sprint S1

Владелец: Codex

Плановые результаты

  • Gmail history-sync changes with fixtures and mutants
  • Skip ledger table (migration in the L-CONN block)
  • Cursor-age alert rule

Dependencies / зависимости

  • depends_on: PRG-01

Доказательства

  • Вершина: 9cfe5ef3e653ad35a7b62a87f7f2ac506d32db61
  • ProofRecord: 01a0fdfc-def3-7e34-81c4-6021757f8051
  • Ревью: 2026-09-30 — Review record: PRG-06 implementation (GPT-PM round 4 on d764d19)
  • Сертификация: 2026-10-02 — Certification record: PRG-06 closure (GPT-PM round 2 on 140d31b)
  • Примечание реестра: ProofRecord 01a0fdfc on 9cfe5ef covers PRG-06 DoD 1-7 and the Global DoD (16-command Linux VPS capture, all exit 0) and supersedes 01a0f7fb and 01a0fdb7. Certified by GPT-PM round 2 (request b7d4e2a8) after round 1 returned two BLOCKERs (G-DOD-06, G-DOD-11) fixed in 36653f4 and 9cfe5ef.

Остаток приёмки

Реестр отмечает закрытие; проверь caveat/исключение в доказательствах.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Сохранять как исторически закрытый; повторно открывать только по конкретному дефекту или изменённому критерию.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • With a messages.get 404 fixture the cursor advances and is persisted; a mutant that removes the skip fails the test.
  • A history.list 404 starts the full-resync path rather than a skip (fixture test).
  • A repeated non-404 per-item error is quarantined after the configured attempts, the cursor advances, and the item is in the skip ledger.
  • Skip-ledger rows are queryable per connection, and a metric counts skips and quarantines.
  • A cursor older than 600 s trips the alert rule in a unit-level rule test with a synthetic cursor; firing on staging is proven in REL-01.
  • A messages.list 404 during full-resync recovery is handled without wedging the cursor: recovery completes or restarts cleanly (fixture test with a mutant that removes the handler failing it).
  • Each malformed history.list, messages.list and messages.get shape, including a present but empty or non-string nextPageToken, fails the page, holds the cursor and records a typed error, while a final page without nextPageToken advances the cursor (REQ-T11-66, one fixture per shape plus the final-page control, each check mutation-tested).

  • The operator has registered the Google staging callback next to the legacy callback without removing it; a real login on the new host succeeds, the session cookie flags are recorded (host-only, no Domain attribute), CSRF positive and negative tests pass, a wrong Origin is refused, and logout then login again works (live test log).
  • OAuth callbacks and post-login redirects use exact host allow-lists and an unknown host fails closed; the CORS allow-list holds exactly the two staging PWA origins of the dual-host window, and a look-alike, a suffix match and an http variant of either are refused with credentials (live-browser test).
  • Re-authentication on the new host is the accepted default: a session cookie from one host is refused on the other, and no cross-host session bridge exists (test).
  • The manifest, icons and service worker are served from the new origin with start_url and scope "/"; offline shows the cached shell and never a stale API response; the update flow prompts; the installed app from the old origin is documented as not migrating and the interim shell tells its user to add the new one (service-worker and install test).
  • Push subscribes anew on the new origin, a gone subscription is removed on the provider response, old-origin subscriptions are bounded and end with the old origin, and the VAPID subject equals the new PWA origin (push test log).
  • The new gateway host answers 403 unauthenticated and works with a valid service token; an invalid HMAC, a replayed nonce and a wrong host or path are refused; the Access audience and key-set state are recorded; the old gateway host stays in the trusted set with a dated removal condition (negative tests).
  • The write owner of every stateful surface during the dual-host window is recorded (one Worker script and one staging database serve both hosts), and a check fails when a second deployment binds the same database (split-brain denial test).
  • The migrations 0116 to 0123 applied with the host change, including the non-additive 0119 and 0120, are recorded as an accepted deviation with the retained pre-change export and its rehearsal as compensating evidence, and from this gate on no release combines a host change with a destructive migration (deviation record and release-check negative test).
  • A physical iPhone completes the ten steps against the staging host (sign in, Add to Home Screen, standalone launch, session present, resume and reload, evidence drill-down, push subscription, push delivery and click landing on the right item, sign out, sign in again), recorded as a ProofRecord with device, iOS version and screenshots (operator action).
  • A 72 h observation window (7 days if the operator prefers) on staging shows zero unhandled errors on the Tier A journeys in an error-rate report, with the nightly staging deploy paused by a recorded freeze flag for the window (report and freeze record).

Replace the capability state vocabulary with the ladder of TDD T9.5 in contract, verifier, registry and UI claims together. The verifier proves REACHABLE through a call-graph edge from a declared entrypoint. Correct the registry (gmail.push_ingest to DECLARED, connector.slack_ingest to its real state, semantic capabilities DEFERRED per D-05). The ops snapshot returns null with a reason for every unmeasured field. Add a head-consistency check for status documents and a ProofRecord schema.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • Removing the only call site of a capability marked REACHABLE or higher fails npm run verify; the mutation run is recorded.
  • gmail.push_ingest is DECLARED, and no capability above IMPLEMENTED lacks a reachability proof.
  • The ops snapshot returns null plus a reason for every field listed as not measured; a test asserts no such field is a number.
  • A status document naming a head other than the verified SHA fails the head-consistency check (negative test).
  • The ProofRecord schema validates this gate's own ProofRecord and the validator refuses each known-bad record in the registry test.
  • The capability registry lists the semantic layer at DECLARED until KNW-01 evidence exists, and a check fails when it is marked higher without a reachable call site (negative test).
  • Every G0-G11 status cell in the status documents cites a file:line or command output and is refreshed at the checked head; the checker fails a cell with no citation (negative test).
  • Adding or certifying a capability or profile registry entry follows a documented process (owner, required evidence, review), and the checker rejects an entry with no certification record (negative test).
  • The capability registry defines seven states (DECLARED, IMPLEMENTED, REACHABLE, CONFIGURED, HEALTHY, PROVEN_ON_RC, LIVE_VERIFIED), and each state above IMPLEMENTED requires its named evidence type; a claim backed only by text presence is refused (negative test).
  • The ProofRecord validator reads a criteria registry (threshold, sample size, measurement source, tool per criterion) and refuses an unknown criterion, a metric below its threshold, a null reported as a number and a record marked ci with no run ID; each refusal has a known-bad-record test and a killed validator mutant.

DAT-01Migration safetyЗАКРЫТ В РЕЕСТРЕ

Смысл / область

Implement TDD T8.5–T8.7: a migration checksum ledger enforced by CI; a mutation class for every migration from 0112 onward; an apply script that refuses CONSTRAINING migrations without a rehearsal and RESHAPING ones without a Time Travel bookmark and a verified export; a rehearsal tool that restores a staging export into an isolated D1, applies the pending chain and writes a parity report. Verify wrangler's out-of-order behaviour. 0112–0123 reached staging before this gate (reports/domain/domain-cutover-live-status-20260929.md:24), so 0119 is not edited (INV-M-06); its RESHAPING class is carried by the ledger (GPT-PM 5d1a7c3e, Q1 B).

MOD-01Modular boundaries in profile-api and packages/domainЗАКРЫТ В РЕЕСТРЕ

Смысл / область

Split services/profile-api/src/index.ts into modules per bounded context and packages/domain into per-context directories, with no behaviour change. Add the lane path-ownership check and a boundary lint (REQ-T5-01). While this gate is open no other lane edits the two exclusive paths. The exclusivity takes precedence over every shared path inside them (services/profile-api/src/router.ts, services/profile-api/src/index.ts and packages/domain/src/legal/store-registry.json): while this gate is open a gate of another lane waits for it rather than editing those files, and the shared-path rules apply to them again once it is closed. The gate is open from the commit that records it in GATE_STATUS.yaml as IN_PROGRESS, BLOCKED or IMPLEMENTATION_APPROVED until its entry is CLOSED, and NOT_STARTED before that. Relocation exception: while MOD-01 is IN_PROGRESS, when a file assigned to a bounded context (the files lists of governance/parallel/MODULE_BOUNDARIES.yaml) is moved from its flat path to its canonical context path, MOD-01 may update the machine-checked references to that file solely by substituting the old repository path with the new repository path, and only in the files of its relocation_paths. No status, semantic evidence, prose claim, policy value, test expectation, owner, gate assignment, requirement status or other content may change under this exception. Narrative and historical references (reports, old decision-log entries, runbooks, reconciliation reports, historical plans and proofs, closed-gate evidence, historical TDD prose, the mutation harnesses of closed gates) keep the path that existed when the evidence was created. Formatter-only whitespace is permitted solely as markdown table cell padding in a relocation_paths .md file, defined precisely: the same number of lines; every differing line a genuine markdown table row outside fenced code (a row of a table with a header row and a valid separator row, never inside a ``` or ~~~ fence, an isolated pipe-prefixed line being no table row), at the same table, row and table size in the substituted base and in the head, starting with a pipe in both versions with the same number of cells (cells split on unescaped pipes outside code spans); each cell's content, with only its leading and trailing spaces trimmed, byte-identical between the substituted base and the head, so the interior of a cell never changes; separator rows keep the cell count and the alignment colons of every cell and may differ only in dash count and padding; every other line byte-identical; at least one approved path substitution in the file; no run of spaces is ever collapsed, and JSON, YAML and source files get no such tolerance. Directory relocation exception: an exact repository directory token ending in / may be substituted only when the frozen MOD-01 relocation census maps that entire recursively tracked source directory to one canonical destination directory (mapped_directories, derived mechanically from mapped_files). A source directory qualifies only when every tracked file beneath it at the census revision is a mapped MOD-01 file and every such file maps beneath the same destination directory; partially moved directories are not eligible, and one source directory maps to one destination directory only. In an implementation pull request a directory substitution is accepted only when no tracked file remains beneath the old directory at HEAD and the mapped destination contains the mapped files; a directory already emptied by earlier merged slices is accepted on the same condition. No prefix, ancestor, descendant, chaining, heuristic or partial-directory matching is permitted. Directory substitutions are otherwise subject to the same relocation_paths byte-equivalence and Markdown-table-padding rules as file substitutions. The legacy gate crosswalk (governance/GATE_CROSSWALK.md only, not companion documents) accepts a cited exact old directory in mapped_directories when no tracked file remains beneath it and the exact mapped new directory contains tracked files, reported as relocated old/ -> new/. The census of the references is governance/parallel/mod01/RELOCATION_CENSUS.json; scripts/verify/check-relocation-references.mjs enforces the exception on every gate/mod-01-* pull request.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The renderer produces every Worker's config for staging, production and preview; CI fails when a committed config differs from the rendered one.
  • Every staging and production resource name carries its environment prefix; the check fails on placeholder values in production configs.
  • A PR produces preview versions of changed Workers bound only to preview data (shown on one PR).
  • Every secret is listed with owner, environment and rotation period; reading an undeclared secret fails a check.
  • An environment flag gates one feature end to end (test).
  • The nightly job deploys trunk to staging and records a release manifest.
  • The independent deletion-ledger mirror store and the HMAC key store exist per environment and are provisioned by the renderer (probe).
  • Feature flags are declared in one registry with an owner and a production default of off, and a report lists the production flag state per release (used by CUT-01).
  • An environment and dependency inventory (hosts, Workers, bindings, third-party services) is generated from the renderer and the registries, and CI fails when it differs from the committed inventory.
  • Every origin and host value is emitted by the renderer per environment from one config source; a hardcoded origin literal fails a lint, and a missing value fails the render (negative test).
  • The nightly staging deploy is paused by a recorded freeze flag while an RC soak (REL-01, REL-03, REL-04) is running, and the pipeline reports a skipped run with the reason (test).
  • A committed origin manifest per environment, generated from the renderer and the registries, records the DNS authority, the mail records, the Pages projects and Worker routes and custom domains, the Access applications with audience, the TLS owner, the OAuth redirect URIs, the cookie topology, the CORS list, the CSP connect list, the service-worker scope, the VAPID subject, the gateway trusted origins, the write owner per state and the rollback owner; CI fails when a field is missing or differs from the rendered configuration (negative test per field family).
  • The gateway host has one name in the manifest and every allow-list, Access hostname and trusted-origin list renders from it; a hand-written second spelling fails the check (negative test).
  • For every state that two hosts can write during a dual-host window the manifest names exactly one write owner, and a manifest with a second owner for one state fails the check (negative test).
  • Every hit of a sibling-project host, a platform default host or a public-app-URL variable is classified as runtime configuration, allowlist, OAuth redirect, PWA metadata, fixture, runbook or historical, historical reports keep their text, and verify:domain-inventory fails on an unclassified hit (negative test).
  • One PWA artifact serves every environment: the staging and production builds differ only in the rendered origin values, and a check comparing the two renders fails on any other difference (test).
  • The secrets inventory lists every platform API token with its scope and purpose and the check fails when a scope exceeds its purpose; while D-30 stands no token carries a mail scope (negative test).
  • A release manifest that carries a hostname or origin change together with a destructive migration or a data-key change is refused (negative test).

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • CONTRACT_REGISTRY.yaml lists every contract with version, owner and consumers; CI fails when a contract changes without a registry update.
  • Each new contract has a Zod schema, fixtures and round-trip tests; v4 NormalizedEvent fixtures still parse and map to v5.
  • A consumer contract test fails when a producer removes a field a registered consumer uses (negative test).
  • No service behaviour changes in this gate.
  • Every contract name in a TDD gate-mapping table appears in CONTRACT_REGISTRY.yaml with an owning gate (script check, negative test).
  • The RPC version-skew rules (callee deploys first, N and N-1 accepted, additive-only within a major) are encoded in the contract test matrix, and a caller-N against callee-N-1 case runs in CI.
  • TrustBoundaryContext is a field of the event, queue-message, storage-write, graph, search and AI-call contracts, and a contract test fails when any of these paths omits it.
  • An independent exact-head review returns APPROVE on the full TrustBoundary, TrustBoundaryContext and PolicyDecision contract set, not only the outbound-send crossing, and the verdict is recorded in the decision log.
  • PrincipalContext is a field of the resolver, event, queue-message and storage-write contracts, and a fixture-level propagation test follows one principal through those four contract types, failing when any contract drops it (runtime propagation is proven in SEC-01).
  • Adapter interfaces for the cell executor, queue, blob store, key provider, AI provider transport, identity provider and telemetry sink are defined in packages/contracts, each with a conformance test (TDD REQ-T25-02); Cloudflare implementations are the canonical MVP1 adapters.
  • ContextAuthorization v1 keeps every ADR-012 guarantee of the current runtime: a lifetime of at most 30 days with renewal as a new epoch, immutability per epoch, a provenance-scope hash recomputed at request time, the exact selected-chat set, an independent operator-approval reference, and LIMITED never authorizing raw-content AI; migration data maps one to one, and tests cover expiry, hash mismatch and LIMITED (contract test).
  • Contract-only versions (types and fixtures, no deployment) of ProcessingActivityRole, CustomerTrustBoundary, CustomerPolicyProfile, AIProviderProfile and BoundaryEnvelope v1 are registered in the contract registry, and the BoundaryEnvelope contract test denies an unknown field, an unknown type and an unknown version on a content-bearing flow.
  • The PrincipalKind enum carries HUMAN, WORKLOAD, CONNECTOR, SUPPORT and CONTROL_PLANE, and a SUPPORT principal has no default grant in the policy table (contract test).

The staging ceiling number is recorded with ENV-01 and the production number with REL-07.
  • Log and audit retention defaults are configured and asserted by a test: audit log at least 365 days, security log 90 days hot, application logs 14 to 30 days, traces 3 to 7 days, metrics up to 13 months, and a read of the audit log is itself audited and cannot export across tenants (test).

S5 — Tenancy schema, scope columns and new PWA foundation

IDT-01Tenancy schema, scope columns and scope enforcementЗАКРЫТ В РЕЕСТРЕ

Смысл / область

TDD T6.1–T6.3 and T8.3, shipped as three separately deployed steps that each get their own ProofRecord: (1) EXPAND — tenants, workspaces, users, user identities and memberships as new tables (NOT NULL), tenant_id and workspace_id added nullable to every legacy business table and backfilled; (2) SWITCH — a dual-compatible code release in which reads and writes carry scope predicates unconditionally, with no feature flag anywhere in the tenant-enforcement code; (3) CONTRACT — additive BEFORE INSERT and BEFORE UPDATE triggers abort a NULL scope on every business table (no table rebuild), applied only after the zero-null and dual-version proofs. Also a nightly null-count check, per-tenant feature-flag overrides (product flags only, never enforcement), data_region reserved, and ADR-015. The migrations are rehearsed on a restored staging copy and applied to staging with a bookmark and an export.

  • Scope predicates on reads and writes are unconditional: no feature flag or configuration value can disable them, and tenant-enforcement code never reads a flag (a CI lint rejects a flag read there). With two tenants provisioned, the full flag matrix leaves a foreign-tenant ID returning 0 rows or 404 (test).
  • ADR-015 is adopted.
  • Staging carries RC0 (REL-01) with migrations 0112–0123 applied before these migrations are applied, so no lower-numbered migration is applied out of order.
  • The three steps ship as separate staging deployments with a ProofRecord each: the previous application version runs against the schema after EXPAND, and after SWITCH, before CONTRACT is applied (old binary by adjacent schema compatibility test); CONTRACT is applied only after the zero-null check and the dual-version proof pass.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Сохранять как исторически закрытый; повторно открывать только по конкретному дефекту или изменённому критерию.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • Every internal service admits only a signed PrincipalAssertion for the expected principal; the functional review records the foreign-principal refusal case per service.
  • Internal Workers are reachable only through service bindings: no public route, and workers_dev is disabled in every rendered config; this is established by source/config review, not a live probe.
  • A replayed assertion on an irreversible operation and an expired assertion are refused in the focused functional acceptance cases.
  • A cross-site mutation without a valid CSRF token is refused in the focused functional acceptance cases.
  • A push endpoint outside the allowlist is refused, and push requests do not follow redirects in the focused functional acceptance cases.
  • No code reads the removed shared token or caller-asserted user header, and unused token-hash bindings are gone by source review.
  • Callees pin the public keys of edge and actions in configuration and a key-set change needs the offline root signature; a callee refuses an assertion signed by a key id added at runtime. Root-key custody is deferred operator state, not a source DoD requirement.
  • The single-use jti applies to every step-up operation, not only irreversible ones, and a replayed role-change assertion is refused; the functional review records the replay case.
  • Asynchronous work carries a signed provenance token issued by ingest or the edge in the queue message, and a consumer refuses a message with a tampered tenant_id, as covered by source review and functional acceptance.
  • No non-operator user is admitted while identity, vault, privacy and ops still share one Worker with the edge signer, unless the pinned keys and the offline root are in place; the residual risk is stated in TDD T18.9.
  • A trust boundary marked PLANNED or BLOCKED remains fail-closed when a merged change relies on it; CI verification is outside this DoD.
  • An EgressEnvelope bound by signature to the payload hash is required at every provider-facing boundary (model, connector send, push, webhook), and a call with a missing, forged or header-only envelope is refused in the focused functional acceptance cases.
  • CORS uses an exact per-environment origin allow-list (no wildcard, no suffix match), and a live-browser test is reviewed in source; a live-browser staging check is outside this DoD.
  • A credential-bearing request to a host or path outside the pinned gateway allow-list is refused, including after a redirect, as covered by the focused functional acceptance cases.
  • While IDT-02 can still switch principals back by flag, assertion subjects are user_id values and a mapping table lets the callee accept the legacy principal for the same person; the mapping is removed when IDT-02 closes, as recorded in the functional acceptance review.
  • An emergency deny path (a root-pre-signed revocation artifact or a config-level deny-list) revokes a compromised pinned key without a key-set ceremony, and the source contract records the stated bound; live runtime enforcement is deferred.
  • Scheduled work items are scoped by a per-tenant provenance token issued by the dispatcher, or the job is declared platform cell-system class with an explicit exemption; the provenance contract is source-reviewed.
  • During a dual-host window the CORS allow-list holds exactly the two PWA origins of that window, and a look-alike, a suffix match and an http variant of either are refused by the source contract; live-browser staging verification is deferred.
  • The gateway trusted-origin set is an exact hostname list; during a dual-host window both gateway hosts are present, each with a dated removal condition, and any other host is refused, including after a redirect.
  • The session cookie SameSite value and prefix derive from the CORS origin list: None only while a cross-site origin is listed, Lax with the __Host- prefix once the list is single-site; the source contract refuses Lax with a cross-site origin listed and None with a single-site list, and the switch to the single-site form is executed at the retirement step of the cutover, not earlier.
  • Every content-bearing or authenticated response sends Cache-Control: no-store, and the endpoint-registry check refuses a route that omits it (REQ-T18-61); registry execution is outside this DoD.

Сверить оставшиеся DoD с доказательствами, выполнить недостающее и зафиксировать точную вершину.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • A fixture connector added as a registry entry plus an SDK module ingests end to end in CI through the expand path, with no edit to a god file.
  • /ingest rejects an event signed with another connection's key, and an old key version after rotation (tests); rotating a runtime key needs the active key, and an owner step-up re-enrolment revokes all keys and bumps authorization_epoch.
  • Replaying an event yields a single ingest record (test), and reconnecting a revoked connection is a REVOKED to CONNECTING transition on the same row with a new authorization_epoch so no duplicates arise.
  • The processor has no per-source branching; the provider comes from the registry (lint and test).
  • Two connections of the same provider in one tenant ingest without a MISMATCH (test).
  • The connection directory answers a Slack team id, a Gmail push subject digest and an ingest key lookup, and a stale cached answer for at most 60 s is safe because a write carries the directory epoch (test).
  • A per-tenant token bucket at ingest limits one tenant's burst without slowing another tenant (test with a 10x burst).
  • Connection SQL lives in repository modules and the connections context exception list is empty.
  • The SourceAdapter contract and certification harness are on the trunk and used by the fixture connector in CI; the check fails if the harness exists only on a side branch (work/connector-reuse-00 is merged or archived per PRG-04).
  • The conformance kit holds a generic history-boundary test: an event with occurred_at before the connection connected_at is refused by /ingest without a valid import run, a reconnect with a new authorization_epoch leaves connected_at unchanged, an out-of-order event around the boundary is decided by occurred_at and epoch and not by arrival order, and a revoked connection ingests nothing (fixture connector test, REQ-T10-78).
  • The provider manifest declares ordering, edit, delete and reconcile semantics, and the kit feeds duplicate, out-of-order, gap, edit and tombstone sequences plus a missed event: it yields one record per item, no cursor regression, a superseded version, a tombstoned item that stays gone after replay, and a missed event repaired by the next periodic reconcile run that never reads before connected_at (fixture connector test, REQ-T10-79).
  • The effective capability set of a connection is recomputed from the granted scopes, policy and source state on every scope, installation, channel or consent change before the next ingest or send: a fixture switched from send to read-only refuses a send and shows NEEDS_REAUTH, and re-consent restores the capability only after the recompute (test, REQ-T10-80).
  • The certification kit fails a fixture connector that lacks any of duplicate delivery, out-of-order, gap recovery, edit and tombstone, periodic reconciliation, semantic mapping and provenance, provider-negative controls, the history boundary, capability recompute or adapter-version compatibility, one removed case at a time (test, REQ-T10-81).
  • The source policy of a connection resolves an operation matrix (index, evidence, local_search, external_ai, outbound_same_source, cross_source_copy) with external_ai and cross_source_copy denied by default, stores it with the event, and is consumed by the AI gateway and the action gate (one test per operation).
  • The activation safety gate of REQ-T10-83 keeps a provider DECLARED and a connection out of ACTIVE while any runtime safety primitive is missing, and a provider call with an unknown outcome is recorded UNKNOWN and resolved by reconciliation before any retry (conformance-kit tests).
  • The processor follows the outcome state machine of REQ-T10-84: per-claim lease token with compare-and-set, heartbeat renewal, PERMANENT_FAILURE to DLQ at any attempt, retry cap to DLQ, and no non-terminal row at or above the cap (state-machine tests with mutants).
  • The reuse catalog validator of REQ-T24-20 runs in CI and fails a copied file without a reuse record, an unresolvable SHA or path, a platform import of AGPL code, a unit whose recorded license obligations are not all met (including an AGPL bridge without its corresponding-source offer), and an activationScope ahead of the ladder state (fixture tree test).
  • The connector suite enforces the security baseline of REQ-T24-21: NetworkTargetGuard on fetched URLs with at most 3 redirect hops, each hop re-resolved and re-checked and https-only, bounded sizes with quarantine, webhook replay refusal and a manifest risk class (one failing fixture per control, plus a public-to-public redirect that is followed).

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • One table-driven suite runs the same transition cases against the domain and the API, and both agree.
  • An item snoozed for 1 h is back in Attention an hour later, and a new material event wakes it early (scheduler tests).
  • Recording a resolution requires a chosen option; the outcome can be recorded later and is included in DSR export.
  • available_actions for each state match the transition table, and the API refuses any other action (test).
  • Existing decisions migrate with mapped states; the rehearsal parity report is attached.
  • The DecisionSchema in decision.ts is removed and the TDD section that contradicted it is corrected; one transition table remains (grep check and AT-T12-12).
  • Evidence snapshot links are immutable and hash-chained, and an evidence-package export is verifiable (test).
  • USER_CREATE and NOT_A_DECISION cancel exist as commands: a user promotes an FYI event to an OPEN user-origin decision, and cancelling a candidate labels it (tests).
  • Idempotency records live in their own table written in the same batch as the operation; a replay after 25 h is treated as new and purge never touches decision_transitions (tests).
  • REPLY is linked both ways (actions.decision_id and an ACTION evidence role); resolve and reply may happen in either order; a default outcome review horizon is set and an outcome-due state is queryable (tests).
  • The scheduler runs from the cron dispatcher at MVP1, and the same job code is callable from a Durable-Object alarm at P4 (interface test).
  • The existing-decision migration was rehearsed and applied to staging with a bookmark and an export.
  • EvidenceSnapshot v2 writes CONTENT_SNAPSHOT as a level above METADATA_SNAPSHOT with the full field model (snapshot version, source identity, payload and metadata hash, capture timestamp, retention-policy reference, key version, integrity-ledger reference, sensitivity class, tenant and workspace ownership); the content is encrypted in the blob store and the row holds only the hash, key version and locator, and an evidence package contains the snapshot content and a LiveSourceReference for each source item, not metadata only (contract test plus export test against the fixture source records, AT-T12-33).
  • The evidence hash-chain head is anchored outside the vendor-writable store (WORM object store or notarization service) at a fixed cadence, and a verification test detects a chain rewrite that the internal chain check alone would accept.
  • Each decision, commitment and milestone created in the fixture workspace carries at least one evidence link, and the MVP1 closure query returns none without evidence.
  • The evidence-level enum is REFERENCE_ONLY, METADATA_SNAPSHOT, CONTENT_SNAPSHOT, FULL_EVIDENCE_PACKAGE in one spelling in the contract, the tables and the tests, the MVP1 default level of every decision_evidence link is at least CONTENT_SNAPSHOT, and a request for a level the profile does not enable is refused by name (spelling check and unit test, AT-T12-34).
  • A decision, commitment or action references a FROZEN snapshot identity (snapshot id, snapshot version and payload hash), never the current source record: after the decision is made the fixture provider edits and then deletes the source item, the evidence package rebuilt from the decision is byte-identical, the edit yields a second coexisting snapshot version that the decision does not reference, an identical re-capture deduplicates, and the LiveSourceReference moves PRESENT, CHANGED, GONE while drill-down still renders the frozen snapshot (mutation test AT-T12-33; a mutant that resolves evidence through the live record fails it).
  • The level-by-sensitivity-by-participation matrix holds: an item that participates in a decision is raised to at least CONTENT_SNAPSHOT whatever its source level, a HIGH item without the special-category condition stays METADATA_SNAPSHOT with the content-not-preserved marker, and a retention policy that requires deletion removes the content and leaves the hashes and a tombstone (full matrix test, AT-T12-34).

Later source and acceptance records include owner-scoped topic drill-down and the PWA drill-down journey; the earlier commit reference above is not a complete inventory. Connector producer adoption, cross-tenant acceptance, deployed delivery and runtime evidence are not established. The four reversible topic commands (merge, unmerge, split, detach) now have an owner-scoped Profile API source path; independent source review found one medium target-version race response mismatch, which was fixed and rechecked with no remaining finding in the changed branch. Command-specific functional acceptance remains NOT_RUN; this does not establish migration, deployed delivery or runtime evidence. The topic-event delivery redrive now maps unavailable delivery/replay tables and replay-reconciliation reads to explicit 503 responses rather than leaking a database exception as a generic 500. The follow-up also maps a thrown active-cell lookup to the declared 503 response. Migration 1491 presence/application and redrive acceptance remain NOT_VERIFIED. Delivery listing, redrive and replay reads now also bind the canonical personal tenant/workspace scope; active-cell resolution uses that same scope. Failed-batch receipt recovery rereads the scoped delivery so its returned status/count reflect current state. These remain source-only changes; migration 1491, focused acceptance and runtime behavior are unverified. No runtime claim.

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The erase RPC refuses a call with no valid PrincipalAssertion or with a replayed jti (test), and the gate checker fails if PRV-01 could close before SEC-01.
  • An erase job removes the subject's rows across the services that exist and writes deletion-ledger receipts that store the HMAC key id and job scope kind; running it again changes nothing (idempotency test), including across an HMAC key rotation.
  • The walker exports and erases every table in the ownership map that exists at this gate, and a table without a handler fails CI (coverage runs in ratchet mode until PRV-02).
  • The retention executor deletes or archives according to the D-06 profile on a clock-controlled fixture, in dry-run mode until the operator enables deletion.
  • Consent records store the capture actor and evidence; the restore-replay refusal flag is set in the control plane before a restore and the drill passes with key-store rows restored to a point before the erasure (test).
  • The processing, role and data-subject inventory in governance/legal has status ADOPTED (not PROPOSED) and covers every table in the ownership map; the checker fails a table with no processing entry.
  • The purpose, lawful-basis and special-category-condition registry has an entry for every processing purpose, and a purpose with no selected basis is disabled (checker); the operator's selections are recorded in LAW-01.
  • A HIGH sensitivity classification or an operator sensitivity label blocks the affected processing path (AI call, export, disclosure) fail-closed, shown by a test per path; a missing classification is treated as HIGH.
  • Consent and privacy preferences are stored per processing purpose for any source, not only Telegram AI consent, and the notice version accepted is recorded (test with a Gmail purpose and a Slack purpose).
  • The export and erase endpoints are the ones PWA-07 calls; a contract test drives export and erasure through the PWA's API client, not only through an internal API or CLI path.
  • The D-06 profile records a retention period for every data class, including the guarded and audit classes, and the executor handles each class (clock-controlled fixture per class).
  • The isolated restore drill with deletion replay runs on a staging-restored fixture at this gate (ProofRecord), and REL-05 repeats it later.
  • Each processing activity in the inventory records customer role, vendor role, provider role, legal-basis owner, instruction source, region, data classes and a data-subject class (ACCOUNT_HOLDER, REFERENCED_THIRD_PARTY, WORKFORCE_MEMBER, CUSTOMER_CONTACT, UNKNOWN), and the checker fails an activity without them.
  • The purpose registry has a closed PROHIBITED class (advertising, cross-tenant profiling, unrelated analytics, monetization, data brokerage, model training on content), and adding a purpose in that class fails the checker without a recorded separate customer agreement (negative test).
  • The privacy_jobs kind CHECK declares EXPORT_SUBJECT, EXPORT_TENANT, ERASE_SUBJECT, ERASE_TENANT, RETENTION, RECTIFY_SUBJECT, RESTRICT_SUBJECT, OBJECT_SUBJECT and OPT_OUT from the first migration, and a profile that does not require a right returns not_supported_in_profile (test, TDD REQ-T17-62).
  • The HIGH sensitivity default covers retention, support access and audit access paths as well as AI call, export and disclosure, and HIGH content is excluded from every diagnostic bundle (test per path).
  • Export chunks expire 72 hours after ready and every chunk of a subject is purged when the erasure job of that subject moves to RUNNING, before its first progress record (REQ-T17-69, clock and purge tests).
  • The erasure barrier is checked by login, device registration, decision_state_audit, source_account_runtime and every mutating route of the subject, answering 423 and writing nothing, for RUNNING, HELD and FAILED jobs (REQ-T17-70, route tests).
  • VERIFICATION_FAILED retries at most twice and the third failure writes an escalation row, stops and raises an alert; PROVIDER_REVOKE_UNKNOWN escalates after its 24 h backoff (REQ-T17-71, injected-writer test).

  • A lint fails when code outside the gateway calls a model binding or provider (negative test).

  • The policy negative suite reports 0 AI-policy violations.

  • The provider registry ships with Workers AI and at least one additional provider (D-18). Each provider is enabled only by per-tenant policy, and before it can receive real content its entry resolves to a reviewed processing-inventory entry in LAW-01 with its data-use, training, retention and location terms (primary source read), a destination and data-class mapping, and the purpose or lawful basis (test: an unpermitted provider receives no content).
  • With Workers AI and a second provider enabled, the processor and decisions services hold no provider binding, key or SDK import, and every model call crosses the private service binding with a valid PrincipalAssertion for ai.invoke (lint and test).
  • The provider-by-provider policy negative matrix fails closed for every wrong tenant, provider, purpose or data-class combination, and disabling the second provider leaves the deterministic and Workers AI paths intact (test).
  • A provider is selectable only when its AIProviderProfile is CERTIFIED on eight criteria, each with a quoted primary-source clause: jurisdiction, data residency, provider region, retention, no-training, no-secondary-use, no-human-review and the prohibited-purpose baseline; a criterion that cannot be verified counts as failed, and one fixture per criterion is rejected (certification test, AT-T16-10).
  • A provider that fails no-training, no-secondary-use or no-human-review is certified for the ROUTINE data class only, a free tier that trains on input is refused for CONFIDENTIAL and HIGH content and the UI names the reason, and a profile past its reverification date degrades to NO_AI with the deterministic path (policy test and e2e, AT-T16-10).
  • The policy input list includes UserAuthority, MinimumNecessaryContext, JurisdictionProfile and the residency and transfer policy, connecting a source is not consent for AI to read it, and each new input has one denial fixture in which the provider double records zero calls (test, AT-T16-11).
  • A user-owned provider key is never held in the vendor ai-gateway Worker or in vendor storage: only platform and tenant credentials live there (configuration and storage scan test, AT-T16-10).
  • A provider profile records endpoint identity, model and API version, region, credential owner, network route, retention, training and secondary-use terms, human-review terms, quota model, failure semantics, contract reference and terms verification date, and a provider entry is usable only while its profile is certified and its verification date is current (test).
  • A provider whose recorded terms do not show no-training, no-secondary-use and no-human-review, or whose terms are unverified, receives no content unless the tenant policy carries an explicit exception with a contract reference (negative test with a provider double whose terms say it may train).
  • The AI policy decision evaluates sensitivity class, jurisdiction profile, residency and transfer policy and the provider region against the tenant data_region, with the reason codes RESIDENCY_DENY, TRANSFER_DENY and SENSITIVITY_DENY, and connecting a source never implies AI eligibility (one test per input).
  • Every call to a tenant-, user- or customer-configured AI endpoint passes the NetworkTargetGuard of REQ-T16-36 in the mode of its trust boundary (VENDOR_SAAS: public destinations only; PERSONAL_LOCAL: plus the configured local endpoint, client process only; ENTERPRISE_DATA_PLANE: plus the customer allowlist, inside the customer boundary only), with metadata addresses always refused, the connection pinned to the checked address, no redirects and bounded sizes and time, at registration and on every call (AT-T16-12 with mutants).

  • During DUAL_HOST exactly one write owner exists per state, and a request that would create a second writer is refused (split-brain denial test).

  • On the new production gateway host an unauthenticated request, an invalid HMAC, a replayed nonce and a wrong host or path are each refused, the Access audience and key-set state for the exact hostname are updated and recorded, and both the old and the new gateway host are in the trusted set with dated removal conditions (negative tests).
  • MX and SPF are checked from two independent resolvers before and after the change and stay as D-30 sets them; DNSSEC is enabled only after the delegation has been stable for a recorded window and not before OBSERVE; HSTS starts with a short max-age and has no includeSubDomains or preload before RETIRE (DNS and header export).
  • No credential-bearing route on the old host is answered by a blanket redirect: each OAuth callback and API route class has an explicit rule (test per route class), and the www host redirects with 308 to the apex with no parking page left (probe).
  • On the production origin, Gmail (an existing connection is visible, re-consent, disconnect and reconnect, reconciliation) and Telegram (revoke and reconnect through the unchanged connector machine, session untouched) are each exercised once (e2e with the real accounts).
  • The production cutover is never released together with a destructive migration or a change to a data key (release-manifest check, negative test).
  • The Telegram API Terms recorded in docs/architecture/EXTERNAL_ASSUMPTIONS.md are re-fetched and their content hash compared before production; a changed hash reopens REQ-T11-68 (recorded check).

Gmail owner-address proof does not prove that a Telegram message in an arbitrary chat is addressed to its account owner. Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • Two Telegram accounts route to their hosts and ingest without a MISMATCH (test).
  • QR login is served by an API and completes with a fixture client on staging with a test account (the PWA screen is PWA-05).
  • A message from a chat without recorded consent from every relevant user never reaches a model (test through AIG-01), and a consent GRANT inserted by an owner principal is refused by trigger.
  • Killing the host mid-batch loses no spooled event (durability test).
  • Send works only through the connector capability with a signed-confirmation fixture, and the host refuses when the draft hash differs from the confirmed one (integration test).
  • A scan of the vault and of host-boundary traffic during QR login and a host move finds no TDLib session or key material outside the host.
  • Consent is captured through a counterparty-authenticated channel with the capture actor recorded, or the chat stays ineligible; host-reported membership is treated as untrusted (tests).
  • Telegram and Gmail classifier rules cover Russian and English, and the corpus records the language mix (fixture check).
  • The consent capture link is bound to the connection, chat and actor, is single-use with an expiry, and the verified Telegram Login user id must equal that actor; a login by a different user id is refused (test).
  • The consent contract and host abstraction support both a hosted runner and a user-run host (D-16), but hosted execution for users other than the operator stays CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE until SCL-04. Before SCL-04 a user-run host works; a hosted request without a recorded terms-of-service check is refused for policy reasons, and with the record it is still refused as CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE and never executes (test). The terms-of-service record is necessary but not sufficient to activate hosted execution.
  • A pre-connection object held in a fixture TDLib-style cache never reaches ingest, evidence, the graph or an AI input with the D-19 import off, and the same object sent by a modified host is refused by /ingest (test, REQ-T10-78, REQ-T11-65).
  • A reconnect, re-authorization or host move bumps authorization_epoch without lowering connected_at, an out-of-order object around the boundary is decided by occurred_at, epoch and connected_at, and revoking the connection closes ingest and AI eligibility at once (tests, REQ-T11-65, REQ-T11-27).
  • Telegram items admitted by a D-19 import run are metadata pointers carrying an import_run_id, never reach an AI input, evidence, the graph or a decision, carry no consent eligibility, and are erased with the connection (test, REQ-T11-65).
  • The Telegram host, tunnel and content-gateway routes send Cache-Control: no-store and pass the REQ-T18-61 registry check.
  • A TDLib history page with any malformed message delivers nothing from that page, keeps the watermark and reports RECOVERY_GAP (REQ-T11-67, filtering mutant fails).
  • The client behaves as Telegram API Terms 1.3 and 1.4 require (REQ-T11-68): the TDLib double records no read, typing or online status suppression and no send without a confirmation record, and a self-destructing message leaves no content in the spool or at the boundary.
  • One TDLib session volume serves exactly one process (REQ-T11-69): a second host on the volume exits on the flock while the first runs, and cleanup with a matching owner label but another mount is refused (Docker lifecycle smoke).
  • The staging host runs under a supervisor that starts it at boot, restarts it after a crash with one restart in flight and bounded backoff, and writes a boot transcript line per start (REQ-T11-70, kill and reboot test).
  • QR control requests are idempotent per (action, flow): replay returns the first result, an old flow logout never satisfies a new flow cancel, cancel terminates the TDLib attempt, and a password-locked flow answers 429 without consuming capacity (REQ-T11-71 regression suite).

  • At least one path is a named claimed path: its slice holds at least the positives its target needs at the 95% Wilson lower bound (73 for a 95% target, 30 minimum) and the corpus minimums are raised to match, so the claim can fail.

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • A request aborted after the server commits shows DELIVERY_UNKNOWN_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED with a reconcile option, never not-sent (test).
  • Reconciliation proves sent or not sent against the provider for Gmail and Telegram fixtures and moves the state (tests).
  • Dispatch without a valid confirmation, with a changed draft hash or with a replayed assertion is refused (tests).
  • actions never calls a provider directly (lint).
  • The reconciliation list is paginated and tenant-scoped.
  • A connector performs a provider call only after actions.claimSend consumes the jti and records PROVIDER_CALLED; a crash between the claim and the provider call leaves the command UNKNOWN and reconciliation resolves it (test with a provider double).
  • Between confirmation and dispatch the draft is held encrypted under the tenant DEK with a TTL, is included in erasure, and no plaintext draft appears in D1, queues or logs after a failed and retried dispatch (scan test).
  • A send needs a session younger than the maximum age plus the signed ActionConfirmation, and one rule is used in TDD T14 and T18 (test).
  • The reply-to-decision contract holds: replying links the action to the decision both ways and a reply plan route exists in the catalogue (test).
  • A repeated dispatch of one ActionPlan with the same idempotency key sends once and returns the first result, and a plan with more than one candidate target is refused as ambiguous until one target is selected (tests).
  • A reconciliation-required action produces exactly one push through the NTF-01 interface with its own generic text (test).
  • At dispatch the source and sensitivity policy, cross-source disclosure, jurisdiction and transfer, and rate and quota are re-validated, and a change since confirmation ends as NOT_SENT with reason POLICY_CHANGED and no provider call (test with a policy change between confirm and dispatch).

  • Pub/Sub delivery supports AUTHENTICATED_PUSH and PULL: the push profile validates the OIDC token (signature, audience, issuer, expiry, service-account email, email_verified); the pull profile uses subscriber IAM and acknowledges only after the durable schedule is written (test for each profile with a duplicate, a retry and a non-2xx or nack).
  • A notification only schedules work: the connector resolves the connection, calls history.list from the last committed history id, processes all pages, then advances the cursor atomically; a stale history id (404) falls back to a full sync (test).
  • The local proof has two levels: an emulator run covering envelope parsing, duplicates, retry, durable enqueue and idempotency, and one real-topic proof under the operator Google Cloud billing account (a real users.watch, a real notification, history.list, cursor commit); the emulator alone does not satisfy this line.

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The breach procedure's notification path was exercised in a tabletop (record).
  • Every processing activity in governance/legal has a lawful basis selected by the operator and the registries are REVIEWED (moved here from the privacy framework).
  • The Google restricted-scope verification status is recorded; if it is not complete, users outside the test list cannot be admitted and REL-10 states that limit.
  • HIGH-sensitivity data follows a fail-closed path when its classification is missing (test).
  • The published terms and privacy notice state that account holders are 18 or older, that child accounts are not supported, that the product is not child-directed and that no targeted advertising is performed; onboarding cannot make a workspace ACTIVE without the 18+ attestation (onboarding test), and a scan finds no advertising or ad-targeting identifier in the client bundle or the API (REQ-T1-05). The later per-jurisdiction legal work only refines this baseline and never weakens it.
  • Every processor and AI provider in the subprocessor list records service, region, data classes, transfer basis (a TransferRecord-lite row), retention and incident-notice terms, and REL-10 refuses non-operator admission while any row lacks one (checker, TDD REQ-T17-61).
  • A DPIA approved by the operator and reviewed by counsel exists for cross-source aggregation, inferred sensitive data and AI profiling before any non-operator admission for an EU or EEA data subject population, and the registry check refuses a high-risk activity without a DPIA reference (TDD REQ-T17-64).
  • A government-request procedure (authorization, jurisdiction, scope, preservation, legal review, audit and transparency policy) is written next to the breach procedure and exercised in the tabletop, and the DPA template states the customer-notice period as an operator-decided value (TDD REQ-T17-60, REQ-T17-64).
  • The terms state that account holders are 18 or older and that child accounts are not supported, child-related content in sources is classed at least CONFIDENTIAL, and the terms and notice state the prohibited-purpose baseline (no advertising, no cross-tenant profiling, no brokerage, no model training on content); a dependency and CSP scan finds no third-party tracker or advertising script (TDD REQ-T17-66).
  • A tenant jurisdiction and residency are contractual attributes chosen at provisioning and never derived from an IP address, and public copy is checked against the controlled-claims list (data never leaves, customer keys, zero-knowledge, no vendor access), failing when a claim has no HEALTHY certified capability behind it (TDD REQ-T25-51).

  • Bookmark and export are recorded, migrations applied, Workers deployed at the RC1 versions, and ops reports the RC1 SHA.
  • Post-deploy compatibility checks (contract N and N-1, smoke tests) pass.
  • Every RESHAPING or CONSTRAINING migration since RC0 was applied to staging with a bookmark and an export, and d1 migrations list shows nothing pending.
  • One canary deploy and one rollback of a Worker version are executed on staging and recorded (ProofRecord with the version IDs before and after).
  • Promotion is refused while any send command is UNKNOWN or unresolved (unresolved UNKNOWN send commands must be 0), and a rollback of a release that touched provider effects is followed by a reconciliation run before traffic resumes (drill record).

  • depends_on: AIG-01

Остаток приёмки

Все критерии ниже остаются в приёмке; ProofRecord не указан.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Начать после выполнения depends_on/can_start_after; выполнить DoD и приложить доказательства.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The semantic packages have package.json files and serve one runtime path with tenant-scoped semantic search.
  • The dormant semantic tables are used by the slice or retired by a recorded decision.
  • The Knowledge view leaves Preview only with its capability evidence.
  • An adoption ADR and a semantic migration inventory (which legacy tables and fields map to which knowledge contracts) are recorded before any semantic cutover work starts, and the checker orders them before the slice's first migration.
  • Every legacy kind is mapped to an UpperKind of the adopted ontology, and a validation test fails on an unmapped or ambiguously mapped kind.
  • crosses, crossedBy and intersects relations return an explanation path and have tests for direct, derived-through-composition and negative cases.
  • The taxonomy engine rejects a cycle formed by the combined scopes of two taxonomies (negative test).
  • Every derived knowledge index is rebuilt from source records and matches the live index (rebuild test).
  • KnowledgeRecord, DocChunk and QueryContract carry a version, are listed in CONTRACT_REGISTRY.yaml and are consumed by at least one real reader in the slice (consumer test).
  • The taxonomy explorer reads the KNW-01 taxonomy engine through the API (e2e, no prototype mock module in the bundle).
  • ADR-039 adopts sections 1 to 20 of PDCC_CANONICAL_TAXONOMY_MODEL_2026-09-18 as its normative input, records every deviation and the superseded legacy material, and decides the UpperKind of Place, Role, Metric, Capability, Quality and Process and the Milestone mapping; a contract-element check fails when ADR-039 or TDD section T26.4 lacks any of CE-01 to CE-14 (test).
  • The semantic migration inventory enumerates every legacy kind, maps owner and assignee fields to responsibleFor or a responsibility assignment with assigned_to kept as a read-side projection, names the resolver fields that feed sameAs and aliasOf without inferring a cross-source identity merge, and rewrites no historical record without a migration map and provenance (inventory test).
  • The UpperKind table of the adopted ontology contains every relation domain and range kind used by the seed relation registry (test).
  • A canonical write with zero, two or an unknown UpperKind is refused (three negative tests), and the UpperKind registry of ten values changes only through an ontology revision ADR.
  • The PLATFORM scope is loaded only from a checked-in, versioned registry file whose count and ids equal the ADR-039 appendix, and a runtime write to it by a tenant or an agent is refused (negative test).
  • A WORKSPACE concept cannot become the parent of a PLATFORM or DOMAIN concept, a concept cannot name a parent in another tenant or another personal scope, and a workspace extension that redefines an UpperKind or adds core relation semantics is refused (negative tests).
  • A concept with two broader concepts is accepted (diamond test), a cycle through a multi-parent path is rejected, and one entity keeps exactly one UpperKind while holding several classifications.
  • A classification whose target concept is a role or a context concept is refused, and role, context, time and epistemic state are stored as relations or attributes (schema test).
  • Adding a DOMAIN, a WORKSPACE and a PERSONAL concept in the fixture is a data-only revision, the migration directory hash is unchanged (test), and a lint fails a code enum for any concept below the UpperKind and core registries.
  • The validator runs its seven steps in the stated order (UpperKind, platform concept, extension under the active revision, cycles, scope and tenant, relation domain and range, assertion with provenance), and a table-driven test covers the four unknown-concept outcomes (known canonical accepted, known extension accepted, unknown user concept becomes a proposal, unknown core kind rejected).
  • Re-parenting a concept creates a new taxonomy revision, an assertion made under revision N still resolves under N, and a crossing derived before the change is unchanged (test); every revision records scope, parent revision, creator, reason and evidence references.
  • A rename keeps the concept id and adds an alias, a merge preserves both ids as aliasOf and supersedes edges and existing assertions still resolve, a deprecated concept rejects new assertions but stays readable for old ones, and the history and why-classified views are answered from stored data (tests).
  • An AI or rule classification proposal is stored as a PROVISIONAL concept or classification proposal and is never returned by canonical queries, promotion needs a taxonomy revision with reviewer identity and evidence, and a path that writes a canonical concept directly is refused (negative test).
  • Every relation type declares an id plus domain_type, range_type, inverse, symmetric, transitive, reflexive, functional, inverse_functional, temporal, contextual and materializable, and the registry test fails a relation whose inverse does not point back, a symmetric relation with a different inverse, and a write whose subject or object kind lies outside its domain or range.
  • The relation registry contains the seed vocabulary of ADR-039 with one test per family (taxonomic, structural, participation and responsibility, dependency, causal and support, planning, context), and a lint rejects a generic relatedTo write when a stronger relation exists in the registry.
  • crosses is asserted or derived and never transitive by itself (negative test), derivation runs only through the composition table and returns its trail, and intersects and crosses are distinct (three tests: two projects that intersect, a person who crosses both, and a meeting that crosses Finance without being partOf Finance).
  • The composition table is a versioned registry, every rule has a positive test, a negative test and a derivation trail, and a rule with an unbounded path length or one that closes a loop is refused.
  • The crossing hyperedge carries subject, contexts, basis, valid interval, crossing kind, derivation and epistemic status, refuses a crossing with no basis, and is rebuilt from source assertions to match the live projection (rebuild test).
  • Every relation assertion carries modality (observed, asserted, planned, required, inferred, predicted, counterfactual or historical), valid time, transaction time, provenance and, when derived, the rule id, and a planned participation is never returned by an observed-participation query (negative test).
  • An as-of query by valid time and an as-of query by transaction time return different results for a late-discovered cancellation (fixture test).
  • The semantic tables gain tenant_id, workspace_id and owner_user_id through a recorded migration that follows the scoped-table policy (expand, backfill, switch, then enforcement by additive triggers or, if the tables are still empty, recreation with NOT NULL columns), and a NULL-scope insert into each of them aborts (test).
  • A legacy semantic row receives a workspace only when its user maps to exactly one workspace of one tenant; every other row moves to a quarantine table with a reason code, is excluded from every read and derived index, is counted in the migration proof, and no default or first-match workspace is ever assigned (fixture with a user that maps to two workspaces).
  • PLATFORM and DOMAIN taxonomy rows live in GLOBAL_CONTROL twin tables registered with a named keying policy and read-only from cells, the ownership map states one scope_policy per semantic table (TDD REQ-T7-29), and a cross-tenant concept read is refused (isolation test with the four fixtures of T17).
  • Erasing a user removes or tombstones the user's PERSONAL concepts, aliases, proposals and classification assertions without breaking revision-chain integrity, and the DSR coverage test lists all six taxonomy tables and the quarantine table (test).
  • The versioned schema of the epistemic and normative graph (epistemic role, claim state, the five verification states with a legal transition table, and NormAssertion with modality, bearer, trigger and discharge) is delivered as contracts listed in CONTRACT_REGISTRY.yaml with no runtime reader, and primaryAssertionRef is an opaque identifier that no KNW-01 code dereferences (a fixture whose reference resolves to nothing still validates).
  • A KnowledgeRecord with two primary assertions, or with a source reference that lacks a source version and a content hash, is refused (two negative tests).
  • The golden questions of the taxonomy and ontology sources (for example commitments with no scheduled work, and meetings whose commitments are still open after 14 days) each compile to a QueryContract and execute on the replayed and redacted fixture, including taxonomy expansion through polyhierarchy and the stop and conflict behaviours; a mock-only run does not satisfy this line.
  • The taxonomy engine, the relation registry and the crossing projection are each consumed by at least one runtime reader of the slice (consumer test) or are listed as DECLARED in the capability registry.
  • The taxonomy explorer e2e asserts browsing the canonical hierarchy with inherited categories, creating a lower concept, rename as alias, deprecate, merge, revision history, why-classified, accept or reject of an AI proposal and semantic groups, and shows six origin badges (PDCC canonical, domain, workspace, personal, AI or rule proposal, deprecated).
  • The schema holds ActivityTemplate with an RRULE-vocabulary recurrence rule, time zone, dated overrides and revisions, and ActivityOccurrence linked by realizes, with a bounded materialization horizon (REQ-T26-19, schema and erasure tests).

LAW-02Residency and the full legal programНЕ НАЧАТ

Смысл / область

Residency and transfers, subprocessor lifecycle, the breach legal clock, AI boundaries, jurisdiction launch gates and the compliance evidence package.

Владелец: Codex

Плановые результаты

  • Jurisdiction launch-gate records

Dependencies / зависимости

  • depends_on: LAW-01
  • depends_on: REL-10
  • depends_on: PRG-09

Доказательства

  • Нет записи статуса; по схеме отсутствие означает NOT_STARTED.

Остаток приёмки

Все критерии ниже остаются в приёмке; ProofRecord не указан.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Начать после выполнения depends_on/can_start_after; выполнить DoD и приложить доказательства.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • Residency and transfer controls, subprocessor lifecycle, breach clock, AI boundaries, jurisdiction launch gates and the compliance evidence package are complete for each launched jurisdiction.
  • A TransferMechanism record has an adopt, review and expire lifecycle, a cross-border transfer is refused fail-closed when its mechanism is missing or expired, and a backup or restore copy never lands outside the tenant's residency region (tests).
  • A subprocessor addition or change triggers the notice and objection lifecycle and takes effect only after the notice period or a recorded objection outcome (test with a subprocessor double).
  • An age and family policy gates the affected processing paths per jurisdiction: a fixture under the jurisdiction's age threshold is refused or routed to the required consent path (test per jurisdiction).
  • A breach record starts every applicable jurisdiction's notification clock automatically and alerts before expiry; a fixture breach shows the deadlines computed per jurisdiction (test).
  • An AI and high-impact processing boundary is enforced per jurisdiction as an AI-gateway policy input; a fixture in a restricted jurisdiction takes the deterministic path (test).
  • Each connected platform's or API's policy terms are recorded with the version reviewed and a compliance verdict, and a connector without a recorded compliant verdict cannot be enabled (test).
  • A market stays disabled until its versioned JurisdictionProfile and required evidence pass; enabling a market with missing evidence is refused (negative test).
  • An outbound disclosure is checked against jurisdiction and consent controls before it leaves the boundary, and a disclosure with no passing check is refused (test).
  • A production compliance evidence package is generated per launched jurisdiction from the registries and ProofRecords, and its completeness check passes.
  • Every pre-v5 evidence and state record is reconciled against the adopted policy (labelled with its lawful basis and jurisdiction, or quarantined), and the query for unlabelled records returns 0.
  • A BreachIncident record carries affected subjects, categories and sensitivity, regions and jurisdictions, detection time, legal clocks, processors, containment, notices, regulator submissions and evidence references, and its incident_kind is one of VENDOR_INCIDENT, CUSTOMER_ENVIRONMENT_INCIDENT, PROCESSOR_INCIDENT_AFFECTING_CUSTOMER_DATA or PROVIDER_OR_SUBPROCESSOR_INCIDENT (test: an unknown kind is refused).
  • Two clock families exist: regulator clocks per jurisdiction where the vendor is a controller, and the contractual processor-to-customer notice clock from the DPA period; a processor-class incident on a BYOC or shared enterprise tenant produces a customer notification deadline and no regulator clock for the vendor (test).
  • A TransferMechanism record has a type taxonomy (including LOCALIZATION_NO_CROSS_BORDER_TRANSFER), a review date, a transfer-impact-assessment reference, supplementary measures and a status, and a TransferRecord (source and destination region, provider, purpose, data category, sensitivity, mechanism, contract reference, policy decision, time) is written before every AI-provider disclosure, support export, cross-region replica, backup copy and failover, and its absence refuses the transfer (test per path).
  • A ResidencyAwareRecoveryPolicy lists permitted and forbidden replica, backup, restore and failover regions, an emergency failover writes a TransferRecord and a return-to-primary step, a localisation profile never fails over out of its region, and the restore drill validates residency as well as recovery (drill).
  • A JurisdictionProfile carries its legal gate set, approved data plane, approved processor and transfer set, feature policy and evidence references, governance/legal/JURISDICTION_REQUIREMENTS.yaml holds the per-jurisdiction prerequisites (UK, US matrix, Russia, mainland China, Japan, Korea, Brazil, India, Canada, Australia, Singapore, Switzerland), and enabling a profile with an unmet row fails (negative test).
  • The profiles JP-RU-LOCAL and JP-CN-MAINLAND exist DISABLED with the mechanism type LOCALIZATION_NO_CROSS_BORDER_TRANSFER, and a tenant jurisdiction and residency are contractual attributes chosen at provisioning and never derived from an IP address (test: the same IP with two profiles gives two results).
  • A DPIA reference is required before a high-risk processing activity runs, and the jurisdiction requirements add the personal-information protection impact assessment for mainland China and its equivalents; an activity flagged high-risk without a reference is refused (test).
  • A GovernmentRequest registry object has the states RECEIVED, LEGAL_REVIEW, SCOPED, PRESERVED, PRODUCED and REJECTED with audit rows; a fixture request cannot be answered without a recorded legal-review approval, and in a BYOC customer-controlled tenant the request is routed to the customer and not answered by a vendor query (tests).
  • A subprocessor change whose contractual notice period cannot be resolved stays blocked, an objection outcome may select an alternative path or disable the capability, and registry rows carry the sub-processing chain and incident-notice terms (test with a subprocessor double).
  • The high-impact uses that the product must not silently become (employment ranking, employee scoring, credit decisions, insurance pricing, medical diagnostics, biometric or emotion recognition, automated legal adjudication, migration or asylum eligibility) are recorded as prohibited purposes in the purpose registry and the AI gateway refuses a purpose from the list (test with a fixture candidate-ranking purpose).
  • The recorded Limited Use rules for Gmail-derived data map to prohibited-purpose rows and to the AI gateway TRAINING_OR_BENCHMARK denial (test with a Gmail-sourced contributor).

  • The load ladder runs on a Paid plan; no load test runs on the Free plan.
  • The existing HARD_ZERO cost-safety behaviour of packages/domain/src/reconciler.ts still passes its current tests unchanged after per-tenant metering is added (test run before and after).
  • Self-service admission is bounded: signup identity is verified, admission and rate limits apply at a scope broader than one tenant id (source network and verified identity), duplicate and automated signups are detected or challenged, a global provisioning budget and a bound on pending tenants are enforced, and no chargeable connector or AI work runs before signup verification completes. An adversarial signup burst from many identities and networks cannot exceed the tenant-creation and cost ceiling, and a partially verified signup can neither connect a provider nor consume AI budget (test).
  • Invitations and self-service signup both reach the same canonical PROVISIONING to ACTIVE flow and write the same tenant audit log entries (test).
  • Above 95% of the recorded spend ceiling the plan tier and add-ons stay unchanged and an operator proposal record is written, and a lint over the deploy and ops scripts fails on a fixture that calls a plan-change or billing-mutation endpoint, so no automatic paid upgrade exists (test, REQ-T27-27).
  • Self-service sign-up requires an attestation that the account holder is 18 or older, stores it with the terms version, and refuses when it is absent; child accounts are not supported (test).
