  • The decision file for the 28 provider rows has named columns for license and distribution compatibility, terms of service and account risk, secret custody, disposition (DIRECT_REUSE, ISOLATED_BRIDGE_SERVICE, REFERENCE_ONLY or REJECT) and a rollback and revoke path, and states that an assessment is not import authority; no code is imported before a DIRECT_REUSE or ISOLATED_BRIDGE_SERVICE disposition is recorded (checker over the decision file).
  • Every connector manifest of this wave declares the data classes it reads and writes against the registered ProcessingActivityRole and CustomerTrustBoundary contracts, and a manifest without the declaration is refused by the certification kit (negative test).
  • Every wave-1 connector and every reused unit carries a REQ-T24-20 reuse record (upstream SHA, path, SPDX license, disposition, license obligations and how each is met), UNKNOWN_LICENSE units are REFERENCE_ONLY, and AGPL units run only as isolated bridge services that meet their own obligations, including the corresponding-source offer (validator output).
  • Every provider of the 2026-09-16 full-catalog staging target resolves to a wave in SRC-02 or EXT-01 or to a recorded rejection in the SRC-02 decision file (REQ-T10-85, checker over the decision file).
SOV-01Personal Sovereign profileНЕ НАЧАТ

Смысл / область

TDD T25 — a personal storage cell, a local vault, local-first sync and trusted-device recovery. Modes (D-29): Personal Private (a local encrypted runtime with storage on the device and, optionally, the user Google Drive app data or OneDrive app folder), Personal BYOC (PDCC deployed in the user own Google Cloud or Azure account, with storage, AI and keys owned by the user) and PDCC Private Vault (vendor-hosted storage with client-side encryption, the vendor seeing ciphertext and routing metadata only); PDCC Hosted is the shared SaaS profile.

Фаза/Phase P6 · Lane L-DATA: Platform data — identity, tenancy, repository layer, migrations, cells · Tier POST · Sprint S20

Владелец: Codex

Плановые результаты

  • Personal Sovereign certification
  • SOV-01.PRIVATE, SOV-01.BYOC and SOV-01.VAULT ProofRecords

Dependencies / зависимости

  • depends_on: SCL-01
  • depends_on: EXT-01
  • depends_on: KEY-01
  • depends_on: DEC-01
  • depends_on: PRV-02
  • depends_on: AIG-01
  • depends_on: LAW-01

Доказательства

  • Нет записи статуса; по схеме отсутствие означает NOT_STARTED.

Остаток приёмки

Все критерии ниже остаются в приёмке; ProofRecord не указан.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Начать после выполнения depends_on/can_start_after; выполнить DoD и приложить доказательства.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The Personal Sovereign certification passes as three separate suites with one ProofRecord each: SOV-01.PRIVATE (personal storage cell, local vault, local-first sync with conflict records, backup and recovery, evidence, disclosure and UX), SOV-01.BYOC and SOV-01.VAULT; no suite inherits another suite PASS, SOV-01.PRIVATE closes first, the BYOC and Vault suites run after it, and a package with any suite ProofRecord missing fails the check (package check, AT-T25-P03).
  • The certification suite includes "no provider refresh token in vendor storage" and "vendor-signed assertion refused by the data plane" for the sovereign profile, and server-side connectors are either moved to the client or declared out of the profile.
  • A Personal Sovereign ADR and profile contract are adopted (ADR status ADOPTED) before any other SOV-01 change merges, and the checker orders them first; the ADR is ADR-043 (personal key recovery, vault format, sync conflict model and AI provider certification) and the profile contract is T25.11.
  • PersonalTrustBoundary is enforced as the profile contract (T25.11, REQ-T25-33) states: it is TrustBoundary v2 with owner type PERSONAL and the storage, compute, identity, key, region and policy-reference fields, a call that would send private content to vendor-operated storage is refused, an unknown boundary reference fails closed (negative tests), and the boundary is no longer PLANNED in the trust-boundary check.
  • A storage-provider abstraction lets the profile select among certified providers without a code change; one contract test runs the same suite against the local, Google Drive and OneDrive providers.
  • A local encrypted transactional store uses envelope crypto (a data key per object wrapped by a user-held key), and a scan of the store finds no plaintext content (scan test).
  • A durable evidence vault stores evidence snapshots as CONTENT_SNAPSHOT or FULL_EVIDENCE_PACKAGE per T12.12 and T25.8, survives restart and device loss through backup restore, and its export is verifiable against the hash chain (test); restore verifies the chain and refuses to open a vault whose chain fails (test).
  • Evidence-policy levels are enforced consistently with the sensitivity gate: an item classified HIGH is handled at the strictest evidence level on every path (test matrix of levels by sensitivity).
  • A certified Google Drive AppData adapter passes the provider certification suite (read, write, conflict, quota, revoke) against a test account.
  • A certified OneDrive AppFolder adapter passes the same provider certification suite (read, write, conflict, quota, revoke) against a test account.
  • Sync durability and degraded states are explicit: offline, quota-exceeded and revoked-provider states each surface in the UI (e2e), every merge conflict creates a ConflictResolutionRecord shown in the Conflict Center, and a causal-sync property test finds no lost update.
  • Storage and AI independence hold: with the profile on, a scan of the vendor's stores after the fixture run finds no private content, and PersonalAIProvider certification passes for each supported provider.
  • An optional local or user-supplied AI path works end to end on a fixture: a request is answered with no vendor model call (network-deny test).
  • A guided Google personal-cloud (BYOC) wizard and a guided Azure wizard each complete setup on a fresh test account without manual config editing (e2e), leaving the profile in HEALTHY state; the Google wizard closes first, the Azure wizard ships dark until it passes, and any other cloud is PLANNED in the capability registry.
  • A Private Vault mode holds content with no central vendor custody: the vault content is unreadable with vendor keys (negative test) and absent from vendor backups.
  • Personal-profile privacy, admin and capability UX is reachable and shows the certified capability state; a capability that failed certification renders as degraded or hidden (e2e).
  • A multi-source disclosure request is honored across every connected personal source only for the destinations the per-destination evaluation allows: a cross-source copy or an ALL send is evaluated per destination, a blocked destination is refused with a visible reason, and ALL resolves to the eligible set frozen in the ActionPlan (test with three fixture sources; multi-target sending stays refused until the ACT-02 scope amendment).
  • A user-controlled recovery secret restores access on a fresh device, and an optional trusted-device pairing path also restores access (both drills recorded); losing both is reported as UNRECOVERABLE, never as an empty account (e2e).
  • The Personal Private profile passes its own certification evidence package (a ProofRecord per criterion) before launch, and a missing item fails the package check.
  • The personal storage abstraction offers Local Device, Google Drive app data, OneDrive app folder, Google Cloud, Azure, PDCC Private Vault and PDCC Hosted Storage behind one interface with a conformance test each, and the evidence retention level is selectable from REFERENCE_ONLY to FULL_EVIDENCE_PACKAGE per source, enforced at ingest (test that a REFERENCE_ONLY source stores no content).
  • For PDCC Private Vault the server holds ciphertext only and the keys stay with the user: a test proves the vendor cannot read plaintext, and losing the recovery secret is stated to the user (certification case).
  • A vault reaches the state RECOVERABLE only after a recovery-material verification challenge or a recorded no-backup acceptance; the negative test tries to reach RECOVERABLE with neither and fails (e2e, AT-T25-P01).
  • Trusted-device pairing uses an ephemeral key and nonce, a compared short authentication string, explicit confirmation by a trusted device, end-to-end encrypted key transfer and a time-limited single-use audited exchange; a replayed pairing, an expired pairing and an unconfirmed pairing each yield no key, and a relay recorded in the fixture never sees key material (negative tests, AT-T25-P02).
  • A scan of vendor key stores and recovery flows finds no escrowed, master or wrapping key that opens any vault fixture, and no support session path exists for the PERSONAL_SOVEREIGN and PRIVATE_VAULT profiles (scan test).
  • With PRIVATE_VAULT active, an AI request routed to the vendor ai-gateway and a server-side search query over vault content are both refused with a capability error, the capability rows vendor_ai_over_vault and vendor_plaintext_search read UNSUPPORTED, and a direct client-to-user-approved-provider call passes with only the minimum context (negative and positive tests, AT-T25-P04).
  • Evidence levels are selectable by source, folder, classification, sensitivity and workflow with the stricter selector winning, an item that participates in a reply or decision is raised to at least CONTENT_SNAPSHOT, the UI warns "Original content may become unavailable" when a source is set to REFERENCE_ONLY, and lowering below the floor records an acknowledgement (test matrix of source level by participation, e2e, AT-T25-P08).
  • With the provider fixture deleting or editing a source item, the vault still serves the snapshot at its recorded level and shows the live-source state as GONE or CHANGED (test).
  • Retention is a user-owned versioned policy with the presets Minimal, Balanced, Archive, Legal/Financial and Custom; each preset has an executor test on a fixture vault, deletion leaves a tombstone, and a snapshot whose active policy requires deletion is deleted even when a decision links it (tests).
  • The UI shows the last confirmed durable-sync time, the unsynced item count and the states NOT_BACKED_UP, SYNC_BLOCKED_QUOTA and SYNC_BLOCKED_AUTH, no item is called durable before the provider acknowledges it, and eviction, interrupted upload, expired authorization and quota exhaustion are test cases (e2e).
  • A device that was offline across an erasure reconnects and its stale copies of the erased records are discarded before upload, and a vault restored from an older backup replays tombstones before it opens for writing (tests, AT-T25-P05).
  • A vault written by format version N opens read-only or migrates on N+1 and is refused by N-1 with a message, never silently forked, and a stricter-wins policy conflict and a deletion conflict are never auto-relaxed by a merge (tests).
  • An isolated restore drill per personal suite records the numeric objectives of REQ-T25-30 as a ProofRecord (recovery point, restore time, profile and release), and a suite without that record is not certified.
  • Key rotation re-wraps data keys without rewriting evidence blobs and the erasure receipt states what remains in provider-side backups; for SOV-01.BYOC, revoking the user KMS key makes the fixture vault unreadable and the vendor identity cannot decrypt (tests).
  • The personal control-plane schema is an allowlist (account, licence, version, deployment mode, non-content health, connector inventory, feature flags, support entitlement) with typed BoundaryEnvelope payloads; an unknown field fails closed and content-leak attempts through error, telemetry, support and update channels fail (negative tests, AT-T25-P07).
  • At least one certified client-side connector (Gmail, the MVP source) ingests into the local vault with the refresh token held only on the device, and every server-side-only connector is listed UNSUPPORTED in the personal CapabilityProfile (test).
  • The Drive and OneDrive adapters request only the application-storage scope, and the storage grant is independent of any source grant (test on the requested scope set).
  • Selecting a personal AI provider shows its retention, training and human-review terms and their verification date; a provider without a current CERTIFIED profile under AIG-01 is not selectable, an expired profile degrades to No AI, a training-on-input tier is blocked for CONFIDENTIAL and HIGH content, and a user-owned key stays on the client or in the user cloud (e2e and policy tests, AT-T16-10).
  • An item the local classifier marks sensitive never reaches an external provider even when one is configured (network-deny test).
  • The BYOC wizard refuses to provision without a numeric budget and 70, 85 and 95 percent alert thresholds, records the user as cost owner, requests only the least-privilege roles it lists, gives the vendor identity no role on the user project, creates exactly its declared resource inventory, is idempotent on re-run, and leaves no orphan resource after a failed run (IAM negative tests and teardown test, AT-T25-P06).
  • Personal-facing copy never says "free forever" and says "can be very low cost" where it speaks of cost; the claims check fails the strings "free forever", "notarized", "never leaves your environment" and "PDCC cannot decrypt" for a profile that lacks the proof, and the profile inventory lists what PDCC still processes and asserts no absence of legal obligations (claims check).
  • Onboarding picks the storage location and the AI provider as two independent choices with Personal Private as the recommended default, the offline mode lists knowledge browsing, historical evidence, taxonomy, graph, plans, local search and local AI with the network blocked, theme and interface settings persist across devices, and the whole-vault export opens in a fresh install and matches its hash manifest (e2e).
  • An MVP1 fixture tenant migrates to Personal Private through export, client re-encryption, verification, a vendor erasure receipt and a rollback window (rehearsal test), and the exit path hands the vault to the user and closes the vendor account (test).
  • The personal client library runs the REQ-T16-36 guard in PERSONAL_LOCAL mode: the configured local endpoint is reachable, any other loopback port, private address and metadata address are refused, and the vendor gateway refuses the same profile (AT-T16-12).

ESH-01Enterprise shared SaaS certificationНЕ НАЧАТ

Смысл / область

TDD T25.9 (REQ-T25-43, ADR-042) — certification of the vendor-operated enterprise profiles: ENTERPRISE_SHARED (shared cells, suite ESH-01.SHARED) and ENTERPRISE_DEDICATED (a dedicated cell or dedicated vendor account, suite ESH-01.DEDICATED). The vendor hosts the content as the customer processor, so this gate adds enterprise identity, administrator roles, customer-retained audit export, policy narrowing, zero-content vendor telemetry, just-in-time support access with a customer-side approver, a recorded legal role and a tenant-isolation proof for each sub-suite. It is independent of EDP-01: a BYOC PASS never depends on it and it never depends on a BYOC or Sovereign PASS.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Начать после выполнения depends_on/can_start_after; выполнить DoD и приложить доказательства.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The two suites ESH-01.SHARED and ESH-01.DEDICATED each write their own ProofRecord on the exact release; a PASS of one is never inherited by the other or by any EDP-01 suite, and the registry has no dependency path between ESH-01 and EDP-01 in either direction (checker: check-profile-matrix.mjs).
  • An enterprise shared SaaS profile contract and ADR-042 are adopted (ADR status ADOPTED) before any other ESH-01 change merges, and the checker orders them first.
  • Enterprise login works through a customer identity provider with SAML and with OIDC (fixture provider), and SCIM deprovisioning revokes the person sessions, workload tokens and connector grants within the stated bound (test measuring the bound).
  • A customer group or attribute maps to a PDCC role through a customer-owned mapping, an unmapped identity is denied, and an MFA or conditional-access claim from the identity provider is enforced and not re-implemented (tests).
  • Enterprise administrator roles map onto the T6.4 permission matrix, can narrow but never widen what the platform allows, and a tenant administrator cannot grant a permission the matrix does not contain (test).
  • A customer-retained audit export carries the audit event set (source access, AI invocation, policy decision, classification change, taxonomy revision, DSR action, deletion, outbound disclosure, administrative change, support access, break-glass access, connector authorization) as a hash-chained log with a manifest signed by the tenant export key, the customer can export it without vendor help, and a privileged audit read writes its own row (test per event kind; an altered export fails verification).
  • A CustomerPolicyProfile narrows the vendor defaults for residency, retention per class, AI provider, cross-source disclosure and support access and never widens them; a test flips each field and observes the changed decision.
  • The vendor-bound telemetry, exception, support and update channels are zero-content by enumerated BoundaryEnvelope schema and a canary planted in a thrown error, a ticket callback and an update-check callback is absent from every vendor-bound message (negative test).
  • Vendor support access is just-in-time and time-boxed, its approval quorum includes a customer-side approver resolved against the customer identity provider, a grant approved only by vendor principals is refused, and a scan finds no standing vendor access to customer content (tests).
  • The legal role is recorded and exercised: the vendor is the customer processor, each tenant processing activity carries a ProcessingActivityRole with a recorded customer instruction, the DPA and subprocessor list are attached to the tenant, and a processor-class incident fixture produces a customer notification deadline and no regulator clock for the vendor (test).
  • The ESH-01.SHARED sub-suite proves tenant isolation on a shared cell: the cross-tenant suite of TST-01, including caches and the AI-context builder, returns 404 or zero rows for every route, RPC and consumer (test).
  • The ESH-01.DEDICATED sub-suite proves isolation of a dedicated cell or dedicated vendor account: no shared cell is reachable from it, its data and keys are separate, and the recorded compute-isolation level (SHARED_CELL, DEDICATED_CELL or DEDICATED_ACCOUNT) is the only level that sales and security text may quote (claims check).
  • ENTERPRISE_DEDICATED optional customer-managed keys use the customer key contract and its five lifecycle tests; a tenant without customer-managed keys uses the platform key hierarchy and the claim "customer keys" is refused for it (claims check).
  • An exit export of an enterprise shared or dedicated tenant contains canonical data, taxonomy, graph relations, audit log, policy profile and source descriptors, restores into a second deployment and passes a row-count and content-hash parity check (test).
  • A capacity proof records the tenant count and rows_read budget of the enterprise cell size on both sub-suites, measured with synthetic tenants (load test with the recorded numbers).
  • An enterprise tenant is provisioned only when a passing ProofRecord of the matching ESH-01 sub-suite exists for the exact release, and the claims check fails "your data never leaves your environment" for these profiles (negative tests).

S22 — Enterprise BYOC and Sovereign profiles

EDP-01Enterprise BYOC and Sovereign profilesНЕ НАЧАТ

Смысл / область

TDD T25 and T18.11 (ADR-041) — the customer-operated enterprise profiles as two SEPARATE certification suites: EDP-01.BYOC (a data plane in the customer AWS, Azure or GCP account with customer IAM, KMS and AI endpoints, enforced egress and zero-content telemetry) and EDP-01.SOVEREIGN (everything inside the customer infrastructure, including a mode with no external egress). Common enterprise prerequisites merge first: the data-boundary ADR, the positive control-plane allowlist and the typed BoundaryEnvelope, the customer key lifecycle contract, enterprise identity, customer-retained audit export, exit and portability, and the signed IaC and package pipeline. Each suite then writes its own ProofRecord; Sovereign never derives a PASS from BYOC and neither suite depends on the other. Enterprise SaaS (shared or managed cloud) is ESH-01, not this gate, and ESH-01 does not depend on EDP-01. The vendor control plane is a positive typed allowlist of operational metadata (D-29).

Фаза/Phase P6 · Lane L-DATA: Platform data — identity, tenancy, repository layer, migrations, cells · Tier POST · Sprint S22

Остаток приёмки

Все критерии ниже остаются в приёмке; ProofRecord не указан.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Начать после выполнения depends_on/can_start_after; выполнить DoD и приложить доказательства.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The BYOC certification suite (suite id EDP-01.BYOC, its own ProofRecord) passes: customer-cloud data plane, customer IAM, KMS and AI endpoints, enforced egress, zero-content telemetry, DSR execution and the Postgres cell adapter with row-level security.
  • The certification suite includes "vendor-signed assertion refused by the data plane" and "no provider refresh token in vendor storage"; the BYOC key set is customer-rooted and the vault lives inside the data plane.
  • An Enterprise Data Boundary ADR and profile contract are adopted (ADR status ADOPTED) before any other EDP-01 change merges, and the checker orders them first.
  • ProcessingActivityRole (controller or processor) is assigned to every processing activity, and policy decisions use it consistently with the CustomerTrustBoundary; a mismatch fixture is refused (test).
  • Control-plane and data-plane contracts are typed and separated per plan section 40.5: a data-plane payload sent through a control-plane interface fails the type check and a lint (negative test).
  • CustomerTrustBoundary and principal context are enforced at the data plane, not only declared: a call without a customer-rooted principal is refused (test), and the boundary is no longer PLANNED in the trust-boundary check.
  • CustomerPolicyProfile drives each enforcement decision it gates (egress, AI endpoint, retention, disclosure); a test flips each policy field and observes the changed decision.
  • Signed, version-pinned BYOC IaC deploys a finite enumerated resource set: the deployed inventory equals the declared list, an extra resource fails the check, and the signature is verified before apply.
  • Customer IAM, KMS and AI credentials are used for all customer data: a scan of the deployment finds no shared vendor credential.
  • A customer-owned AI endpoint and profile is selectable per customer and enforced: a call to a non-selected endpoint is refused (test).
  • Egress-deny is enforced for the enterprise and BYOC profile: a probe from the data plane to a non-allowlisted host fails, and the allowlist equals the declared set.
  • The zero-content telemetry and support schema is adopted for the enterprise profile with a content-leak negative test over the BYOC telemetry and support channel, beyond the shared refusal in packages/observability/src/telemetry.ts.
  • A JIT break-glass path grants time-boxed support access whose approval quorum includes a customer-side approver resolved against the customer identity provider, a grant approved only by vendor principals is refused, the customer can revoke it at any time, and every grant and use is written to the customer tamper-evident audit log; the default diagnostic bundle contains no content (scan test).
  • DSR export, retention and deletion execute inside the customer's boundary for the BYOC profile: a fixture erase leaves zero rows, writes a receipt in the customer's own ledger and leaves no vendor-side copy (test).
  • The enterprise admin UX exposes policy, capability and trust-boundary controls, distinct from the generic admin UX, and each control changes the enforced state (e2e).
  • A multi-source disclosure request is enforced across every connected enterprise source with one semantics for every profile: each destination has its own state (READY, SENDING, SENT, SKIPPED_POLICY, SKIPPED_UNAVAILABLE, FAILED_RETRYABLE, FAILED_TERMINAL, UNKNOWN_RECONCILE), an ineligible destination is skipped and reported per destination, and the request as a whole is never shown as success unless every target is SENT (test with three fixture sources, one made ineligible before execution).
  • No customer deployment is offered unless the BYOC certification suite has a passing ProofRecord for the exact package version; the offer path refuses otherwise (negative test).
  • On-prem and Sovereign packages are signed and version-pinned before distribution, and installing an unsigned or unpinned package is refused (test).
  • An enterprise legal and security evidence package is generated per certified customer deployment, and its completeness check passes.
  • The Sovereign or On-Prem mode defaults to external_content_egress DENY, external_ai DENY and vendor_content_access DENY until the customer explicitly allows otherwise, and a certification case proves a fully egress-free deployment ingests, decides and exports (test with all egress blocked).
  • The Sovereign certification suite (suite id EDP-01.SOVEREIGN) has its own ProofRecord on the exact package version and its own tests run: it may reuse common enterprise components, but its PASS is never derived from the EDP-01.BYOC ProofRecord, and neither suite lists the other among its dependencies (checker: check-profile-matrix.mjs; fixture with the BYOC ProofRecord deleted leaves the Sovereign result unchanged).
  • Ordering inside the gate is mechanical: the common enterprise prerequisites (the data-boundary ADR, BoundaryEnvelope, key lifecycle contract, identity, audit export, signed IaC pipeline) merge before either suite, the two suites then run in either order, and Sovereign or On-Prem packaging is not offered without an EDP-01.SOVEREIGN ProofRecord for that exact package version (negative test on the offer path).
  • ESH-01 and EDP-01 are independent: the registry has no dependency path between them in either direction, and the profile matrix maps ENTERPRISE_BYOC to EDP-01.BYOC and ENTERPRISE_SOVEREIGN to EDP-01.SOVEREIGN, one certification gate for each profile and no suite shared between them (checker: check-profile-matrix.mjs).
  • The vendor control plane accepts only the enumerated typed fields of the ADR-041 allowlist (tenant or deployment id, software version, license state, feature flags, region, connector-type inventory, health, aggregate non-content metrics, billing meters, routing hints, support entitlement, policy-metadata references, upgrade state), and content, embeddings, excerpts, prompts, provider payloads, free-text diagnostics and raw personal data are forbidden unless a profile contract adds a typed field by an ADR-041 amendment (test: the allowed-field list in code equals the ADR list).
  • Every message from a customer data plane to the vendor plane is a BoundaryEnvelope v1 (boundary_schema_version, message_type, field_classifications, content_allowed, purpose, origin, destination, policy_decision_id, expiry, integrity) and the receiver rejects an unknown field, type, version or an expired envelope fail closed (negative tests on the telemetry, exception, support, update and billing channels).
  • Negative tests prove that a vendor identity cannot query the customer content store, cannot retrieve source bodies and cannot retrieve embeddings, and that a canary planted in a permitted string field, a thrown error message, a support ticket callback and an update-check callback is absent from every vendor-bound message and vendor store.
  • The users, memberships, connection directory, provider subject digests and runtime public keys of the BYOC and Sovereign profiles are data-plane tables and are absent from the vendor control plane (schema check of the vendor-plane store).
  • A customer key contract is adopted (ADR-041): a customer key-encryption key in the customer KMS or HSM wraps per-object data keys, the vendor holds no decrypt grant, and the eight lifecycle stages (create or enrol, use, rotate, dual-read migration, revoke, crypto-erasure, backup and restore, disaster recovery) each have an owner and a recorded outcome; the claim "customer keys" is refused in any capability or marketing text until its ProofRecord exists (claims check).
  • Key lifecycle test 1: use of a revoked old key version fails with the typed refusal KEY_REVOKED and reads no data, and re-enabling the same key version makes the data readable again (test).
  • Key lifecycle test 2: after a rotation the current key version works and data written before the rotation still reads, and dual-read migration retires the old version only after re-wrapping completes (test).
  • Key lifecycle test 3: an unavailable KMS fails closed with no read, no write of new protected data and no fallback to another key, and service resumes after the outage without manual repair (test).
  • Key lifecycle test 4: a restore of a backup whose original key no longer exists fails closed, names the missing key and decrypts nothing with a different key (test).
  • Key lifecycle test 5: tenant deletion by crypto-erasure leaves the protected data unreadable and writes a receipt that states what remains in backups and how derived indexes were rebuilt or deleted (test); a completeness check fails the suite when any of the five key cases is missing.
  • KMS region and jurisdiction awareness: a key outside the profile permitted regions is refused at enrolment, a failover key region outside those regions is refused, and a key unreachable from the recovery region fails closed and never falls back to a vendor key (test per case).
  • Enterprise identity works through the customer identity provider: SAML and OIDC login with a fixture provider, SCIM deprovisioning that revokes sessions, workload tokens and connector grants within the stated bound, a customer group or attribute mapped to a PDCC role with deny on unmapped, an MFA or conditional-access claim enforced and not re-implemented, and a vendor SUPPORT principal with no default data-plane grant (tests).
  • The customer-retained audit event set (source access, AI invocation, policy decision, classification change, taxonomy revision, DSR action, deletion, outbound disclosure, administrative change, support access, break-glass access, connector authorization) is written into the customer data plane, exported by the customer without vendor help with a hash-chained log and a manifest signed by a customer-held key, and a privileged audit read writes its own audit row (test per event kind; export altered by one entry fails verification).
  • An exit export contains the canonical data, taxonomy, graph relations, audit log, policy profile, source descriptors and the vault export re-wrapped to a customer-supplied key, restores into a second deployment of any certified profile and passes a row-count and content-hash parity check, with derived indexes excluded and rebuilt (test).
  • Each processing activity carries customer role, vendor role, provider role, purpose, scope, legal-basis owner, instruction source, region and data classes; processor-mode processing without a recorded customer instruction is refused at the data plane, and billing, security telemetry and service administration are the only vendor activities allowed to write to the vendor control plane (tests).
  • The CustomerTrustBoundary schema carries tenant, deployment mode, cloud account, regions, networks, identity provider, KMS owner, data stores, approved processors, approved AI providers, allowed and denied egress and support access mode, and a PolicyDecision for a boundary-crossing operation records the seven transfer conditions (destination approved, purpose authorized, data category eligible, jurisdiction permits, customer policy permits, auditable, security controls satisfied) and denies when any is unevaluated (truth-table property test).
  • The CustomerPolicyProfile names its fields (residency, key owner, AI provider and training policy, retention per class including embedding, backup, connector token metadata and outbound execution log, cross-source disclosure, support access and diagnostic bundle), and the flip test enumerates each of them and observes the changed decision.
  • In BYOC and Sovereign the AI gateway provider set equals exactly the customer AIProviderProfile list, a vendor-operated provider is unreachable by construction, and with the customer endpoint down the deterministic path runs with zero vendor-provider calls (test with provider doubles).
  • The BYOC infrastructure-as-code declares its ingress set and a scan finds no public ingress other than the declared user-facing endpoint, egress can be forced through a customer proxy and the egress probe passes through it, and a private endpoint to the customer AI endpoint is a capability with its own ProofRecord.
  • The vendor can publish an update notice but cannot apply one (no control-plane credential exists in the data plane), SBOM and provenance are verified before deployment (a tampered artifact is refused), an update runs a canary that asserts the boundary, key-revoke and restore invariants and rolls back to the previous verified artifact on failure (drill), and the Sovereign profile verifies and applies an offline signed bundle (tests).
  • The EDP-01.SOVEREIGN suite also proves: telemetry disabled or customer-proxied, customer-local backup and restore with tombstone replay, a scan finding no vendor credential and no vendor network path into production, and a dependency closure check showing no runtime fetch from a public registry, CDN or provider and a deployed inventory equal to the declared set.
  • Before certification each customer-operated profile records a numeric RPO and RTO target, the key owner and the configuration owner; an isolated restore in the customer cloud replays the customer deletion ledger before serving; the ledger mirror and the HMAC key store are customer-side; the restore drill also validates residency; and provisioning refuses without a customer-set numeric cost budget (drill and negative test).
  • A machine-readable provider certification matrix (governance/data/ENTERPRISE_PROVIDER_MATRIX.yaml) has one row per supported cloud, runtime, KMS or HSM, identity type, AI endpoint class, support path and update path; a combination not in the matrix cannot be provisioned (negative test) and each row stays PLANNED until its ProofRecords exist.
  • The BYOC certification suite has one named case per line of the source data-boundary acceptance list (vendor cannot query content, retrieve source bodies or retrieve embeddings; cannot invoke AI outside the approved provider; unapproved egress blocked; approved AI egress works; customer key revoke blocks protected reads; customer SSO policy applies; support user has no standing access; break-glass requires approval; diagnostic export contains no content; deletion propagates; audit records all privileged actions) and a completeness check fails when a case is missing.
  • The diagnostic bundle is produced by a customer-run command against a field allowlist, requires a recorded customer review before export, is transferred encrypted and one-time, expires, and excludes HIGH-classified content unconditionally (tests including an expiry test).
  • The enterprise admin UX shows an effective-policy view per data class (region, key, AI provider, export, retention) and a list of every allowed egress path generated from the enforced allowlist, and the view equals the enforced state (test).
  • A BYOC erase job lists every processor that received the subject content with an acknowledgement state (ACKED, NOT_APPLICABLE_ZERO_RETENTION, FAILED_RETRY, MANUAL) and cancels queued AI work for the erased subject; restriction and rectification requests execute inside the customer boundary on the customer instruction (tests).
  • A paid enterprise offer needs a versioned CommercialProfile record (segment, tiers, pricing meters, support and SLA package, DPA package references) and the offer path refuses without it (negative test); the record does not block architecture or certification work.
  • Inside the customer boundary the REQ-T16-36 guard runs in ENTERPRISE_DATA_PLANE mode: an allowlisted private endpoint is reachable, a private address off the allowlist and the metadata address are refused, and an allowlisted name that re-resolves off the allowlist fails at call time (AT-T16-12).

PRG-00Preserve unpushed integration workЗАКРЫТ В РЕЕСТРЕ

Смысл / область

A push-only safety step that needs no CI and no decision: push an annotated backup tag at the current head of the integration branch so that its unpushed commits exist on the remote before any consolidation begins. Nothing is merged, rebased, deleted or force-pushed.

Фаза/Phase P0 · Lane L-GOV: Program governance and legal · Tier A · Sprint UNASSIGNED

Владелец: Codex

  • governance/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md (first entry: the pre-consolidation head)

The implementation is limited to installing Chromium and its system dependencies after npm ci and before npm run verify, with policy coverage of the exact command and ordering.

Фаза/Phase P0 · Lane L-OPS: Environments, CI/CD, observability, release and DR · Tier A · Sprint UNASSIGNED

Владелец: Codex

Плановые результаты

  • CI workflow installs the lockfile-resolved Chromium revision and its Linux system dependencies
  • Workflow policy test that rejects a missing, incorrect, or misordered browser-install step
  • Exact-head native verify-linux evidence

Dependencies / зависимости

  • depends_on: PRG-08

Доказательства

  • Нет записи статуса; по схеме отсутствие означает NOT_STARTED.

Остаток приёмки

Все критерии ниже остаются в приёмке; ProofRecord не указан.

Статус DoD-пунктов отдельно в реестре не ведётся; ниже показан полный объём, не индивидуальные статусы.

Следующее действие

Начать после выполнения depends_on/can_start_after; выполнить DoD и приложить доказательства.

Владелец: Codex

DoD — точные критерии мастер-плана

  • The verify-linux workflow runs the lockfile-resolved Playwright CLI with `--with-deps chromium` after npm ci and before npm run verify, so both the browser binary and its Linux shared-library dependencies are installed; no package or runtime dependency is added.
  • The workflow policy test fails if the Chromium install step is missing, omits `--with-deps`, uses a different command, appears before npm ci, or appears after npm run verify.
  • On the exact implementation head, native GitHub checks governance, plan and verify-linux pass, including the existing Chromium-backed browser test; the recorded evidence identifies that SHA and its native run.

Глобальный DoD для всех гейтов

Эти 14 требований применяются к каждому гейту дополнительно к его собственному DoD.

IDТребование (канонический текст)
G-DOD-01**Scope.** The diff stays inside the gate's declared scope and its lane's paths, plus the shared paths it declares. Paths outside the lane are declared in the gate's `cross_lane_paths`.
G-DOD-02**Functional coverage.** Changed behaviour has written acceptance cases and a focused functional/e2e review at sprint end by the designated reviewers. Small unit checks may be used during development; full automated suites, CI tests and mutation runs are outside this DoD.
G-DOD-03**Source review.** The exact commit receives the required focused specialist review and GPT review. `npm test`, Vitest suites, pytest, lint, typecheck, build, CI and other prohibited automatic checks are not DoD requirements.
G-DOD-04**Migration source.** Every new migration has a lane-block number, a header (lane, service, mutation class, cross-lane dependencies) and a ledger entry. It follows the reviewed forward-only/expand-contract design. Rehearsal, remote apply and migration verification are deferred operational actions, not DoD requirements.
G-DOD-05**Tenancy.** Every new table declares a `scope_policy` in `TABLE_OWNERSHIP.yaml` (`TENANT_WORKSPACE`, `TENANT`, `GLOBAL_CONTROL` or `CELL_INTERNAL`). Cell tables and tenant-owned control tables carry the scope columns their policy requires; a `GLOBAL_CONTROL` table names its keying and authorization policy instead of carrying fake scope columns. Tenant-scope enforcement is never feature-flagged. New queries go through repositories with ScopeContext. New routes, RPC methods, consumers, crons and ID-addressed resources are recorded in the four inventories, and every asynchronous entry declares its tenant source.
G-DOD-06**Contracts.** Changed boundaries use versioned contracts registered in the contract registry, with written consumer acceptance cases reviewed in the focused functional cycle. Breaking changes follow the N/N-1 window (TDD T9).
G-DOD-07**Security.** No new public endpoint unless the gate's scope requires one. Trust-boundary changes carry threat notes. No secrets in code, logs or errors.
G-DOD-08**Privacy.** New tables are in the ownership map with a data class, a retention class and erasure coverage (TDD T17.4).
G-DOD-09**Observability.** New asynchronous paths and external calls define their metrics, failure states and alert contract in source. Unobserved runtime measurements remain explicitly deferred and are never reported as zero.
G-DOD-10**Capabilities.** The capability registry reflects the implemented source state; no staging, provider or runtime capability is claimed without actual observation.
G-DOD-11**Flags.** Incomplete user-visible behaviour merges behind a flag that defaults to off in production.
G-DOD-12**Documentation.** Touched Master TDD sections are updated in the same PR, or an ADR records the change. The decision log records durable decisions, and the gate registry records status.
G-DOD-13**Review.** Internal specialist review runs before external review. GPT-PM gives one sweep, then there is one remediation, then one verification round, and APPROVE is on the exact head, unless the operator has decided D-26 to allow an approval to survive a rebase that a dependency-impact check proves irrelevant. Findings deferred at the round cap go to the backlog with a named owning gate.
G-DOD-14**Evidence and rollback.** The change note records the exact commit, actual review results, observed checks and unobserved/deferred items. Source rollback and migration follow-up are documented. Prohibited runtime commands, staging migrations and ProofRecords for unobserved runtime results are not required.

Свежесть, расчёты и ограничения

806 DoD-строк текущего каталога; план HTML от 17.10 содержал старый счёт 776. Разницу не считаю прогрессом.

Владелец: Operator только для manifest.operator_only; иначе Codex. GPT-PM — независимый reviewer/approval, не отдельный владелец.
